# BorrowLoop — Annotated Claude Code Session Log: US-1 Google Authentication and Community Access

**Author:** Pranav Sirnapalli
**Project:** BorrowLoop, Sprint 1 — US-1 / Issue #6
**Branch:** `feature/us1-google-auth-community`
**Pull request:** [PR #37 — US-1 Google authentication and community access](https://github.com/aarushikaushikk/BorrowLoop/pull/37)
**Assignment focus:** Explore → Plan → Implement → Commit, with evidence of human review and testing
**Tool:** Claude Code v2.1.330 (Opus 5.5 in the exported session)
**Evidence source:** `BorrowLoop-US1-Claude-Session.txt` (10,959 lines), reproduced below in full and in order

## How to read this log

- **Transcript blocks** are the actual exported Claude Code conversation, including the prompts, reports, tool traces, and any collapsed tool calls. I have not invented missing tool output.
- **Annotation (my approach)** is my explanation of what I was trying to do at each step. I added it afterward; it is not part of the original session.
- The export begins after several early US-1 checkpoints; it is **not** a complete record of the first four commits. I use GitHub PR history to corroborate those commits instead of pretending their original Claude prompts are in this export.
- My local Windows home-directory prefix is shortened to `~` for privacy. Otherwise the transcript is reproduced in its original order and wording.
- The session documents several intermediate failures and provisional decisions. Those are kept because they show the development process; they should not be mistaken for the final state.
- All Git mutations were performed by me, not Claude Code. The transcript's commit suggestions are not Git commits.

## 1. Starting state and an early correction

### Checkpoint 4: protected pages and the streamed HTTP status problem

**Original Claude Code transcript — `BorrowLoop-US1-Claude-Session.txt`, lines 1–206**

~~~~text
 ▐▛███▛█   Claude Code v2.1.330
▝▜██████▀  Opus 5.5 · Claude Pro
 ▝▝   ▝▝   ~\Documents\BorrowLoop


● Checkpoint 4 is done: signed-out visitors are redirected to /sign-in, the
  sign-in page and the protected /communities page are in place, and a fake
  session cookie exposes nothing. All 190 tests pass with 100% coverage, and
  every validation you listed passed. Nothing is staged or committed.

  Checkpoint 4, Issue #6. Branch confirmed as feature/us1-google-auth-community.
  HEAD was cb8e078 with a clean tree when I started.

  A correction to the earlier 403 plan

  The installed Next docs (loading.md, "Status codes") say that with
  cacheComponents on, a request-time check inside a page runs after the shell
  has been sent. The status stays 200, even with instant = false. I was wrong in
  Explore and Plan when I said forbidden() plus instant = false would give a
  real 403.

  - For this checkpoint: a signed-out visitor with no cookie still gets a real
    307 from the proxy. A request with a fake cookie gets 200 with a streamed
    redirect and no authenticated content.
  - For D5 (admin 403): a real 403 needs a check that runs before streaming.
    That means the proxy (which would then need to verify the session and look
    up membership) or a route handler that returns 403. This needs a team
    decision before we build admin pages.

  RED → GREEN → REFACTOR

  Stage: RED
  npm test (from C:/): exit 1: 68 failed, 122 passed (190). tsc passed.
  Notes: 12 new tests passed against the stub (which matched every path and
    let every request through). Every failure was Not implemented or an
    assertion mismatch, such as 200 to be 307 or the home page not
    redirecting.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Stage: GREEN
  npm test (from C:/): exit 0: 14 files, 190 passed
  Notes: Lint showed 3 warnings for unused parameters in the test mocks.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Stage: REFACTOR
  npm test (from C:/): exit 0: 190 passed
  Notes: Typed the mocks, which cleared the warnings. Moved the sign-in path
    logic so the proxy, requireUser and Auth.js all use SIGN_IN_PATH and
    signInPathFor from src/lib/auth/routes.ts. Added the actions and content
    component to coverage.

  Files

  New:
  - src/proxy.ts: denies by default. Its matcher skips only /sign-in,
    /api/auth/*, _next/static, _next/image and static files. It checks only that
    a session cookie is present, never verifies it, and exempts public paths in
    code too, which prevents redirect loops.
  - src/lib/auth/proxy-rules.ts:
    - isPublicPath.
    - hasSessionCookie, which recognizes authjs.session-token, its __Secure-
      form, and the chunked .N names. I confirmed these names in the installed
      @auth/core source.
  - src/lib/auth/routes.ts: SIGN_IN_PATH and signInPathFor.
  - src/app/(auth)/sign-in/page.tsx: the responsive sign-in card with a
    "Continue with Google" button. The search params are read inside Suspense.
    An Auth.js error shows a generic message and never echoes the error value.
  - src/app/(auth)/sign-in/actions.ts: signInWithGoogle. The provider is fixed
    on the server, and the destination goes through parseCallbackUrl.
  - src/app/(app)/communities/page.tsx and communities-content.tsx:
    - requireUser("/communities") runs inside Suspense.
    - The page shows the user's name and a sign-out button.
    - It labels listings as "pending database integration."
  - src/app/(app)/communities/actions.ts: signOutAction, which signs out and
    redirects to /sign-in.
  - Tests: src/proxy.test.ts (28), proxy-rules.test.ts (25), routes.test.ts (7),
    sign-in/actions.test.ts (5), communities.test.ts (6), home.test.ts (1).

  Modified:
  - src/app/page.tsx: now just redirect("/communities").
  - src/app/layout.tsx: BorrowLoop title and description.
  - src/lib/validation/auth.ts and its test: added parseSignInSearchParams (8
    new tests).
  - src/server/auth/config.ts and session.ts: now use the shared sign-in path
    helpers.
  - vitest.config.mts: added src/app/**/actions.ts and src/app/**/*-content.tsx
    to coverage.

  Validation

  ┌─────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │     Command     │                        Result                         │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ npm test        │ exit 0: 14 files, 190 passed                          │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ npm run         │ exit 0: 100% statements (66/66), branches (38/38),    │
  │ test:coverage   │ functions (25/25) and lines (64/64)                   │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ npm run lint    │ exit 0                                                │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ npx next        │ exit 0                                                │
  │ typegen         │                                                       │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ npx tsc         │ exit 0                                                │
  │ --noEmit        │                                                       │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                 │ exit 0 with no auth variables and no .env* files. /   │
  │ npm run build   │ is static, /communities and /sign-in are partial      │
  │                 │ prerender, and the proxy is registered.               │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ npm ci          │ exit 0                                                │
  │ --dry-run       │                                                       │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ git diff        │ exit 0                                                │
  │ --check         │                                                       │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ git status      │ 7 modified files and 14 new files, as listed above    │
  │ --short         │                                                       │
  └─────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  HTTP checks (next start)

  Request: GET /, no cookie
  No auth variables: 307 → /sign-in?callbackUrl=%2F
  Throwaway AUTH_SECRET + AUTH_TRUST_HOST=true: 307, same
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Request: GET /communities, no cookie
  No auth variables: 307 → /sign-in?callbackUrl=%2Fcommunities
  Throwaway AUTH_SECRET + AUTH_TRUST_HOST=true: 307, same
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Request: GET /c/abc/admin, no cookie
  No auth variables: 307 → …callbackUrl=%2Fc%2Fabc%2Fadmin
  Throwaway AUTH_SECRET + AUTH_TRUST_HOST=true: 307, same
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Request: GET /sign-in
  No auth variables: 200. Has the button, and the hidden callbackUrl is
    /communities.
  Throwaway AUTH_SECRET + AUTH_TRUST_HOST=true: 200, same
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Request: /sign-in?callbackUrl=https://evil.example
  No auth variables: 200. Hidden callbackUrl is /communities.
  Throwaway AUTH_SECRET + AUTH_TRUST_HOST=true: Same
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Request: /sign-in?callbackUrl=/c/abc
  No auth variables: 200. Hidden callbackUrl is /c/abc.
  Throwaway AUTH_SECRET + AUTH_TRUST_HOST=true: Same
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Request: /sign-in?error=<script>…
  No auth variables: 200. Generic message, nothing reflected.
  Throwaway AUTH_SECRET + AUTH_TRUST_HOST=true: Same
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Request: /next.svg, /favicon.ico, no cookie
  No auth variables: 200
  Throwaway AUTH_SECRET + AUTH_TRUST_HOST=true: 200
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Request: /sign-in with a fake cookie
  No auth variables: 200, no redirect loop
  Throwaway AUTH_SECRET + AUTH_TRUST_HOST=true: 200
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Request: / with a fake cookie
  No auth variables: 307 → /communities
  Throwaway AUTH_SECRET + AUTH_TRUST_HOST=true: 307
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Request: /communities with a fake cookie
  No auth variables: 200 + streamed
    NEXT_REDIRECT;…/sign-in?callbackUrl=%2Fcommunities and a meta-refresh
    fallback. No "Signed in as" or "Sign out".
  Throwaway AUTH_SECRET + AUTH_TRUST_HOST=true: Same. The server log shows
    JWTSessionError: Invalid Compact JWE. Chunked fake cookies get the same
    result.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Request: /api/auth/providers
  No auth variables: 500 (UntrustedHost)
  Throwaway AUTH_SECRET + AUTH_TRUST_HOST=true: 200, lists only google

  Two body matches looked suspicious and I traced both:
  - evil.example appears only in the router payload's record of the current
    request URL. It isn't in any link, form value or redirect target.
  - "Your communities" on the fake-cookie response is only the static page
    <title>.

  The throwaway secret was generated in memory for that one process. It was
  never written anywhere, and it isn't a Google credential.

  Limitations and open items

  1. D5 needs a new decision (correction above).
  2. A forged cookie gets 200, not 307. No content leaks, but HTTP clients see
     a 200. Only a stronger proxy check would change that.
  3. Hosting outside Vercel needs AUTH_TRUST_HOST=true or AUTH_URL. On Vercel
     it's detected automatically.
  4. Real Google sign-in and sign-out haven't been run. That needs real
     credentials in your own .env.local. The redirect URI is
     http://localhost:3000/api/auth/callback/google.
  5. The two page files aren't unit tested. I checked them only through HTTP.
     The logic inside them is unit tested, and Playwright can cover the pages
     later.
  6. The template SVGs in public/ are now unused. I left them rather than delete
     files.
  7. Still open from earlier: the session ID is a random value per sign-in until
     the adapter lands, the 70% threshold isn't enforced, the Windows
     drive-letter problem remains, and next-auth is a beta.

  Suggested commit message: feat(auth): add sign-in page, protected communities
  route, and proxy redirect

  I'm stopping here for your review.

~~~~

**Annotation (my approach):** This export picks up after some earlier US-1 work, so I didn't try to make it look like the whole feature started here. The important issue in this checkpoint was the 403 response. Claude realized that a protected page could still send HTTP 200 after streaming started. I wanted us to fix the actual status code, not just show an access-denied message on the page.

     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Area: Dependencies                                                      │
     │ PR #36 has: @prisma/* 7.10.1, cloudinary, zod 4.6.7, dotenv, tsx,       │
     │   allowScripts                                                          │
     │ US-1 currently has / expects: next-auth beta.32, server-only, zod       │
     │   4.6.7                                                                 │
     │ Effect: Combine. Both use the same zod.                                 │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Area: src/lib/db.ts                                                     │
     │ PR #36 has: No import "server-only"                                     │
     │ US-1 currently has / expects: Rule: database access stays               │
     │   server-only                                                           │
     │ Effect: Wrap it (CP-6)                                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Architectural decisions that need your approval                         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ I'll treat each recommendation as a proposal until you approve it.      │
     │ Every schema change goes through Aarushi, and every migration needs     │
     │ your explicit approval.                                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ D-A. Auth.js identity strategy                                          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Recommended: the Auth.js Prisma adapter, keeping the JWT session      │
     │   strategy.                                                             │
     │   - The adapter links each Google account to one User row through       │
     │     Account(provider, providerAccountId). token.sub then becomes the    │
     │     stable User.id (a cuid).                                            │
     │   - Because the strategy stays JWT, there's no Session table and the    │
     │     proxy's cookie names don't change.                                  │
     │   - Membership is still read from the database on every request, never  │
     │     stored in the token. docs/security.md §4 requires this.             │
     │   - Cost: Aarushi adds an Account model, with a migration you approve.  │
     │     VerificationToken is only needed for email sign-in, so it can be    │
     │     skipped.                                                            │
     │   - It also adds one dependency, @auth/prisma-adapter. Before           │
     │     installing it I'll confirm it works with Prisma 7's prisma-client   │
     │     generator and its custom output path. If it doesn't, I'll stop and  │
     │     ask.                                                                │
     │ - Fallback if the dependency doesn't fit: a small hand-written adapter  │
     │   in src/server/auth/adapter.ts. JWT mode only needs createUser,        │
     │   getUser, getUserByEmail, getUserByAccount, updateUser and             │
     │   linkAccount. It still needs the Account table but adds no package.    │
     │ - Not recommended: no adapter, upserting User by email in the jwt       │
     │   callback. It needs no schema change, but identity would be keyed on a │
     │   changeable email address rather than Google's stable account ID.      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ D-B. Membership status                                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Recommended: add status MembershipStatus @default(ACTIVE) to          │
     │   Membership, with the values ACTIVE | SUSPENDED | REMOVED. Add PENDING │
     │   only if D-C includes admin approval.                                  │
     │   - This keeps the existing decideMemberAccess/decideAdminAccess rules  │
     │     and their tests unchanged.                                          │
     │   - It supports the security rule that suspended members lose access    │
     │     immediately.                                                        │
     │   - Existing seed rows default to ACTIVE.                               │
     │ - Interim option if the schema change is delayed: the Prisma lookup     │
     │   treats "row exists" as ACTIVE, and the domain types stay as they are. │
     │   This is a small change later.                                         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ D-C. How users join a community, and multi-community membership         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Recommended: join with an invite code, and allow membership in        │
     │   several communities.                                                  │
     │   - Add a unique, nullable Community.joinCode column.                   │
     │   - A signed-in user enters the code on /communities. A Server Action   │
     │     validates it with Zod and creates an ACTIVE MEMBER membership.      │
     │     Joining a community you're already in is idempotent.                │
     │   - URLs are scoped by /c/[communityId], so there's no "current         │
     │     community" state to switch.                                         │
     │   - First admin: created through the seed script or a one-off script    │
     │     that you run. There's no community-creation UI in US-1.             │
     │   - Rate-limit or lock out repeated wrong codes later. For US-1, use    │
     │     high-entropy codes (at least 10 random characters) and a generic    │
     │     "Invalid code" message.                                             │
     │ - Alternatives: admin approval (needs PENDING plus an admin UI, which   │
     │   is US-8 scope) or both.                                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ D-D. Real HTTP 403 (and 404) mechanism                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Recommended: an authorization gate in src/proxy.ts for                │
     │   /c/:communityId/..., plus the same check repeated in each page and    │
     │   action.                                                               │
     │   - Proxy runs in Node by default (proxy.md line 257) and can return a  │
     │     Response directly (proxy.md, "Producing a response").               │
     │   - For /c/:id/admin(/*), the proxy resolves the session, runs the      │
     │     existing authorizeCommunity(..., level: "admin") against the Prisma │
     │     lookup, and returns:                                                │
     │     - 403 for a member who isn't an admin                               │
     │     - 404 for a non-member or an invalid ID                             │
     │     - a pass-through for an admin                                       │
     │   - For other /c/:id/* paths, it does the same at member level and      │
     │     returns a real 404 for non-members.                                 │
     │   - Pages and Server Actions still call the guards themselves, so a     │
     │     matcher mistake can never expose data.                              │
     │   - Cost: one indexed membership query for each /c/* request in the     │
     │     proxy.                                                              │
     │ - Rejected: forbidden() plus experimental.authInterrupts. Under         │
     │   cacheComponents it runs after the shell has streamed, so the status   │
     │   stays 200 (the "Status codes" section of loading.md, verified in      │
     │   CP-4). It also needs an experimental config change.                   │
     │ - Sub-decision: the 403 body. I propose a small HTML page, "You don't   │
     │   have access to this page", sent with Cache-Control: no-store, instead │
     │   of the current JSON body from accessDeniedResponse. JSON stays for    │
     │   API routes.                                                           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ D-E. Test tooling versions                                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Recommended: adopt main's vitest 5.1.1, which is now possible because │
     │   main moves @types/node to 24. Pair it with @vitest/coverage-v8        │
     │   5.1.1.                                                                │
     │   - Drop vite-tsconfig-paths in favor of main's resolve.alias, which    │
     │     means one fewer dependency.                                         │
     │   - This changes runner versions, which you told me not to do without   │
     │     approval.                                                           │
     │   - I'll check again whether the Windows lowercase-c: double-load bug   │
     │     still happens on 5.x.                                               │
     │ - Alternative: keep 4.1.12 and pin main back down. Aarushi's tests      │
     │   would then need to pass on 4.x.                                       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ D-F. Integration tests against a real Postgres                          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - docs/testing.md asks for authorization tests against real database    │
     │   rows. Recommended:                                                    │
     │   - A separate *.int.test.ts suite that is skipped unless               │
     │     TEST_DATABASE_URL is set.                                           │
     │   - In CI, a Postgres service container with prisma migrate deploy.     │
     │   - This needs a standing approval for migrations against the throwaway │
     │     test database only.                                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ D-G. Where the CI changes go                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Recommended: a separate small PR after #35 merges. It adds npm test   │
     │   and coverage (with the threshold) to ci.yml, plus the integration job │
     │   from D-F, and removes the stacked-branch triggers. This keeps CI      │
     │   separate from US-1, as you asked earlier.                             │
     │ - Alternative: one final CI commit on this branch.                      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Checkpoints                                                             │
     │                                                                         │

     │ drive). You do every Git mutation, including the merge itself. After    │

     │ CP-5. Integrate main once #35 and #36 are merged                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Goal and acceptance criteria:                                         │
     │   - The feature branch contains the latest main.                        │
     │   - All 190 existing tests pass, along with Aarushi's tests             │
     │     (cloudinary, item-photos).                                          │
     │   - Lint, tsc and build pass, and the build works without DATABASE_URL  │
     │     or auth variables.                                                  │
     │   - npm ci --dry-run exits 0.                                           │
     │   - No auth code is lost.                                               │
     │ - Prerequisite: I read git log origin/main and confirm both PRs have    │
     │   merged. If they haven't, I stop.                                      │
     │ - Approach:                                                             │
     │   - You run git fetch and then git merge origin/main. I recommend a     │
     │     merge over a rebase because the branch is already pushed.           │
     │   - I edit only the conflicted files, and you review and commit:        │
     │     - package.json: combine the scripts (keep test:coverage,            │
     │       postinstall, db:*) and the dependencies, apply the D-E versions,  │
     │       keep allowScripts.                                                │
     │     - package-lock.json: don't hand-merge it. Take main's version, then │
     │       run npm install --package-lock-only --ignore-scripts (approval    │
     │       needed, since it changes dependencies) and check with npm ci      │
     │       --dry-run.                                                        │
     │     - vitest.config.mts:                                                │
     │       - Keep main's resolve.alias and the prisma/**/*.test.ts include.  │
     │       - Add back restoreMocks and the coverage block.                   │
     │       - Add src/server/dal/** to coverage.                              │
     │       - Exclude src/generated/**.                                       │
     │     - eslint.config.mjs: ignore both coverage/** and src/generated/**.  │
     │     - .gitignore: take main's version.                                  │
     │   - Run npm ci. Its postinstall runs prisma generate, which works       │
     │     without a database URL according to prisma.config.ts.               │
     │ - Verify: npm ci, npm test, npm run test:coverage, npm run lint, npx    │
     │   next typegen, npx tsc --noEmit, npm run build, npm ci --dry-run, git  │
     │   diff --check.                                                         │
     │ - Risks:                                                                │
     │   - The Vitest 5 upgrade could change mock behavior or bring back the   │
     │     Windows drive bug.                                                  │
     │   - postinstall could fail on Windows.                                  │
     │   - @emnapi entries in the lockfile could drift again.                  │
     │ - Commit: your merge commit, Merge main (CI, Prisma, Cloudinary) into   │
     │   feature/us1-google-auth-community. If the config needs reconciling    │
     │   after that, a second commit: chore(test): reconcile Vitest config     │
     │   with Prisma setup.                                                    │

     │     expects.                                                            │

     │   your explicit approval with npm run db:migrate -- --name              │
     │   us1_auth_membership, against your local database.                     │
     │ - Verify: npx prisma validate, npx prisma generate, tsc, and npm test.  │
     │   The migration SQL is reviewed by you and Aarushi.                     │

**Annotation (my approach):** I asked Claude to use Plan Mode before doing more coding because US-1 depended on the CI and database PRs. The first plan brought up some real questions: how Google accounts would map to users, how memberships would work, and how we'd return 403 and 404 responses. I wanted those decisions sorted out before implementation.

     │                                                                         │
     │ - Recommended: the Auth.js Prisma adapter, keeping the JWT session      │
     │   strategy.                                                             │
     │   - The adapter links each Google account to one User row through       │
     │     Account(provider, providerAccountId). token.sub then becomes the    │
     │     stable User.id (a cuid).                                            │
     │   - Because the strategy stays JWT, there's no Session table and the    │
     │     proxy's cookie names don't change.                                  │
     │   - Membership is still read from the database on every request, never  │
     │     stored in the token. docs/security.md §4 requires this.             │
     │   - Cost: Aarushi adds an Account model, with a migration you approve.  │
     │     VerificationToken is only needed for email sign-in, so it can be    │

     │     ask.                                                                │

     │   in src/server/auth/adapter.ts. JWT mode only needs createUser,        │

     │     - 403 for a member who isn't an admin                               │

> The revised plan looks good. PR #35 is confirmed merged, while PR #36 is
  still pending.

  Please give me a concise final summary of the revised plan, including the
  architectural decisions, remaining dependencies, and the first implementation
  checkpoint.

  Do not make any additional changes to the plan unless you identify a critical
  issue. Do not implement code or perform Git mutations.

  We will begin implementation after PR #36 is merged and I explicitly
  authorize it.

● No critical issues came up, so the plan is unchanged since your last review.
  It is saved at ~\.claude\plans\golden-yawning-newell-of-92908f77-
  c103-4207-a864-35c2207d85ca.md.

  Current state

  - PR #35 (CI, from chore/github-actions-ci) is merged into origin/main as
    614e8f6. Its workflow runs lint, type-check and build, with no test step.
  - PR #36 (Prisma + Cloudinary) is not merged. It was branched from main before
    #35 landed, and both PRs changed package-lock.json. Expect a lockfile
    conflict that Aarushi will need to resolve before #36 can merge.
  - The US-1 branch has four commits and 190 passing tests.

  Decisions that need your approval

  #: D-A
  Decision: How signed-in users get a stable ID
  Recommendation: Use Auth.js's Prisma adapter and keep JWT sessions. Each
    Google account maps to one User row, so the session's user ID becomes the
    permanent database ID. This needs a proposed Account table and the
    @auth/prisma-adapter package. If that package doesn't work with Prisma 7,
    I'd write a small adapter myself instead.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-B
  Decision: Membership status
  Recommendation: A proposed Membership.status field (ACTIVE, SUSPENDED,
    REMOVED, default ACTIVE). If that's delayed, the interim rule is that
    having a membership row counts as active.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-C
  Decision: Joining a community
  Recommendation: Build it only if you confirm it's in Issue #6's scope. If
    so: an invite code (a proposed Community.joinCode), membership in several
    communities allowed, and the first admin created by the seed script. If
    not: a read-only list of the user's communities.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-D
  Decision: Real HTTP 403
  Recommendation: The proxy checks membership on /c/:id/... requests: 403 for
    non-admins on admin pages, 404 for non-members. Pages and actions repeat
    the check. Page-level forbidden() can't help here, because it still
    returns 200 when pages stream.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-E
  Decision: Vitest version
  Recommendation: Move to 5.1.1 with matching coverage tooling, but only once
    compatibility checks pass. Otherwise stay on 4.1.12.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-F
  Decision: Tests against a real database
  Recommendation: A separate integration suite that runs only against a local
    or CI throwaway Postgres, never Neon. Its migrations need a standing
    approval limited to that test database.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-G
  Decision: CI changes
  Recommendation: A separate PR from main. US-1 gets only the coverage
    threshold.

  Remaining dependencies

  1. PR #36 merges into main. I'll confirm that read-only before starting CP-5.
  2. You and Aarushi agree the schema proposals (Account, Membership.status, and
     joinCode if needed). After that, you approve a migration against a local
     database only.
  3. You decide whether joining is in scope.
  4. Real Google credentials go in your own .env.local, for the manual sign-in
     check in CP-7.

  Checkpoint order

  1. CP-5: merge main into the US-1 branch.
  2. CP-6: schema changes (Aarushi).
  3. CP-7: stable user IDs through the adapter.
  4. CP-8: membership checks against the database, plus joining if it's in
     scope.
  5. CP-9: each community page shows only that community's listings, to members
     only.
  6. CP-10a: a throwaway test of whether Auth.js, Prisma and server-only modules
     work inside the proxy.
  7. CP-10: the 403 and 404 responses, proven with real status codes from a
     production build.
  8. CP-11: documentation, with CI in its own PR.

  First checkpoint: CP-5, merging main

  - Gate: after you fetch, I confirm that both PR branches are part of
    origin/main. If #36 hasn't merged, I stop.
  - Before the merge: I record every existing test name and the list of auth
    files.
  - You run git merge origin/main.
  - I resolve the conflicts:
    - package.json: combine both sets of scripts and dependencies.
    - package-lock.json: start from main's version and regenerate it, which
      needs your approval.
    - vitest.config.mts: use main's alias setup and add back our coverage and
      mock settings.
    - eslint.config.mjs: ignore both coverage/** and src/generated/**.
  - Checks:
    - All 190 tests must still exist and pass, along with Aarushi's tests.
    - No auth file may be lost or changed.
    - Then npm ci, npm run test:coverage, npm run lint, npx next typegen, npx
      tsc --noEmit, npm run build (with no database URL or auth variables set),
      npm ci --dry-run and git diff --check.
  - Suggested commit: your merge commit, plus chore(test): reconcile Vitest
    config with Prisma setup if the config needs reconciling after the merge.

  I'll wait for #36 to merge and for your go-ahead before changing anything.

~~~~

**Annotation (my approach):** I went through the plan and pushed back on a few points. I corrected the CI branch, asked Claude to check whether the other PRs had actually merged, and kept schema changes tied to Aarushi's review. I also wanted a small feasibility test before we committed to the proxy approach. That saved us from treating an untested idea as a decision.

     Ground rules (from your review)

     - Scope. Only Issue #6 and its acceptance criteria. Community joining goes
       only as far as the agreed requirements need. No admin features beyond the
       placeholder page needed to prove the 403.
     - Schema. Account, Membership.status and Community.joinCode are proposals.
       I won't edit prisma/schema.prisma or create or run any migration until
       you and Aarushi have agreed and you approve.
       - Never run migrations against the shared Neon database.
       - Migrations run only against a local or throwaway test database, and
         only with your approval.
     - Preserve existing work. All existing auth code and all 190 tests must
       survive the integration.
     - CI. CI changes stay out of US-1 wherever practical.
     - Workflow. Explore → actual Plan Mode → Implement → Commit, with at least
       three meaningful commits. You do every Git mutation.

     Architectural decisions that need your approval

     I'll treat each recommendation as a proposal until you approve it. Every
     schema change goes through Aarushi, and every migration needs your explicit
     approval.

     D-A. Auth.js identity strategy

     - Recommended: the Auth.js Prisma adapter, keeping the JWT session
       strategy.
       - The adapter links each Google account to one User row through
         Account(provider, providerAccountId). token.sub then becomes the stable
         User.id (a cuid).
       - Because the strategy stays JWT, there's no Session table and the
         proxy's cookie names don't change.
       - Membership is still read from the database on every request, never
         stored in the token. docs/security.md §4 requires this.
       - Cost: Aarushi adds an Account model, with a migration you approve.
         VerificationToken is only needed for email sign-in, so it can be
         skipped.
       - It also adds one dependency, @auth/prisma-adapter. Before installing it
         I'll confirm it works with Prisma 7's prisma-client generator and its
         custom output path. If it doesn't, I'll stop and ask.
     - Fallback if the dependency doesn't fit: a small hand-written adapter in
       src/server/auth/adapter.ts. JWT mode only needs createUser, getUser,
       getUserByEmail, getUserByAccount, updateUser and linkAccount. It still
       needs the Account table but adds no package.
     - Not recommended: no adapter, upserting User by email in the jwt callback.
       It needs no schema change, but identity would be keyed on a changeable
       email address rather than Google's stable account ID.

     D-B. Membership status

     - Recommended: add status MembershipStatus @default(ACTIVE) to Membership,
       with the values ACTIVE | SUSPENDED | REMOVED. Add PENDING only if D-C
       includes admin approval.
       - This keeps the existing decideMemberAccess/decideAdminAccess rules and
         their tests unchanged.
       - It supports the security rule that suspended members lose access
         immediately.
       - Existing seed rows default to ACTIVE.
     - Interim option if the schema change is delayed: the Prisma lookup treats
       "row exists" as ACTIVE, and the domain types stay as they are. This is a
       small change later.

     D-C. How users join a community, and multi-community membership

     - Scope note. The Issue #6 title says "join a community", but the
       acceptance criteria only need membership to exist. If the team defers
       joining, CP-8 shrinks to a read-only community list, with membership
       seeded locally. Joining is built only if you confirm it's in scope.
     - Recommended, if joining is in scope: join with an invite code, and allow
       membership in several communities.
       - Add a unique, nullable Community.joinCode column.
       - A signed-in user enters the code on /communities. A Server Action
         validates it with Zod and creates an ACTIVE MEMBER membership. Joining
         a community you're already in is idempotent.
       - URLs are scoped by /c/[communityId], so there's no "current community"
         state to switch.
       - First admin: created through the seed script or a one-off script that
         you run. There's no community-creation UI in US-1.
       - Rate-limit or lock out repeated wrong codes later. For US-1, use
         high-entropy codes (at least 10 random characters) and a generic
         "Invalid code" message.
     - Alternatives: admin approval (needs PENDING plus an admin UI, which is
       US-8 scope) or both.

     D-D. Real HTTP 403 (and 404) mechanism

     - Recommended: an authorization gate in src/proxy.ts for
       /c/:communityId/..., plus the same check repeated in each page and
       action.
       - Proxy runs in Node by default (proxy.md line 257) and can return a
         Response directly (proxy.md, "Producing a response").
       - For /c/:id/admin(/*), the proxy resolves the session, runs the existing
         authorizeCommunity(..., level: "admin") against the Prisma lookup, and
         returns:
         - 403 for a member who isn't an admin
         - 404 for a non-member or an invalid ID
         - a pass-through for an admin
       - For other /c/:id/* paths, it does the same at member level and returns
         a real 404 for non-members.
       - Pages and Server Actions still call the guards themselves, so a matcher
         mistake can never expose data.
       - Cost: one indexed membership query for each /c/* request in the proxy.
     - Rejected: forbidden() plus experimental.authInterrupts. Under
       cacheComponents it runs after the shell has streamed, so the status stays
       200 (the "Status codes" section of loading.md, verified in CP-4). It
       also needs an experimental config change.
     - Sub-decision: the 403 body. I propose a small HTML page, "You don't have
       access to this page", sent with Cache-Control: no-store, instead of the
       current JSON body from accessDeniedResponse. JSON stays for API routes.

     D-E. Test tooling versions

     - Recommended: adopt PR #36's vitest 5.1.1, which is now possible because
       #36 moves @types/node to 24. Pair it with @vitest/coverage-v8 5.1.1.
       Adopt them only after compatibility checks pass:
       - npm ls shows no peer conflicts.
       - All 190 existing tests and Aarushi's tests pass from C:/.
       - Coverage runs.
       - The behavior of vi.mock("server-only") and vi.hoisted doesn't change.
       - If any check fails, I stop and report, and the versions stay as they
         are.
       - Drop vite-tsconfig-paths in favor of main's resolve.alias, which means
         one fewer dependency.
       - This changes runner versions, which you told me not to do without
         approval.
       - I'll check again whether the Windows lowercase-c: double-load bug still
         happens on 5.x.
     - Alternative: keep 4.1.12 and pin main back down. Aarushi's tests would
       then need to pass on 4.x.

     D-F. Integration tests against a real Postgres

     - docs/testing.md asks for authorization tests against real database rows.
       Recommended:
       - A separate *.int.test.ts suite that is skipped unless TEST_DATABASE_URL
         is set.
       - In CI, a Postgres service container with prisma migrate deploy.
       - This needs a standing approval for migrations against the throwaway
         test database only, either local Docker or the CI service container.
         Never Neon.

     D-G. Where the CI changes go

     - Recommended: a separate small PR (#35 has now merged). It adds npm test
       and coverage (with the threshold) to ci.yml, plus the integration job
       from D-F, and removes the stacked-branch triggers. This keeps CI separate
       from US-1, as you asked earlier.
     - Alternative: one final CI commit on this branch.

     Checkpoints

     checkpoint I stop and report the evidence.

     CP-PRE. Pure community route matcher (before the database work; can start
     now)

     - Why now: CP-10's proxy gate needs to know, from the path alone, whether a
       request is a community page or a community admin page, and which
       community it targets. That logic is pure, needs no database, and doesn't
       depend on PR #36. It's part of US-1 (AC3 and AC4), not a separate
       feature.
     - Goal and acceptance criteria:
       - matchCommunityPath(pathname) in src/lib/auth/proxy-rules.ts returns a
         discriminated result:

     type CommunityRouteMatch =
       | { kind: "none" }                                   // not under /c/
       | { kind: "invalid" }                                // under /c/ but
     malformed → future gate answers 404
       | { kind: "community"; communityId: string; level: "member" | "admin" };
       - Treat malformed paths under /c/ as "invalid" rather than null. That way
         the future gate fails closed and returns a 404 instead of letting them
         through.
       - none (unrelated): /, /communities, /sign-in, /api/auth/..., /cx/abc,
         /api/c/abc, and /C/abc. Next routes are case-sensitive, so /C/abc never
         reaches /c/.
       - invalid:
         - /c, /c/, /c//admin, and a community ID that fails the existing
           communityIdSchema (for example bad id, a.b, or longer than 64
           characters).
         - Empty segments in the middle of the path.
         - A segment that decodes to . or ...
         - Percent-encoding that can't be decoded (%E0%A4%A).
         - A path longer than 2048 characters.
       - member: /c/abc, /c/abc/, /c/abc/items/123, /c/abc/administrator (whole
         segment only), and /c/abc/items/admin ("admin" isn't the first segment
         after the ID).
       - admin: /c/abc/admin, /c/abc/admin/, and /c/abc/admin/reports/1.
         - It also matches when the first segment after the ID is "admin" in any
           capitalization (/c/abc/ADMIN) or percent-encoded (/c/abc/%61dmin).
           It fails closed: treating more paths as admin only makes the check
           stricter.
       - Each segment is decoded once with decodeURIComponent before it's
         checked, which matches how Next routes. The ID is validated after
         decoding.
     - Files:
       - src/lib/auth/proxy-rules.ts: add matchCommunityPath, the
         CommunityRouteMatch type and COMMUNITY_ROUTE_PREFIX = "/c".
         - Reuse communityIdSchema from src/lib/validation/community.ts.
         - The level type is declared locally as "member" | "admin", which is
           structurally the same as AccessLevel. Importing it from
           src/server/domain/access.ts would pull server-only into a module the
           proxy imports.
       - src/lib/auth/proxy-rules.test.ts: add a describe("matchCommunityPath")
         block with table-driven it.each cases for each of the five groups.
         Include the ID returned for valid paths, so a test can confirm it's the
         decoded, validated ID and nothing more.
     - Explicitly not touched:
       - src/proxy.ts and its behavior.
       - Any Prisma, database or @/lib/db import.
       - The schema, migrations, package.json, vitest.config.mts and CI.
     - Approach: write the tests first and run them to confirm they fail on the
       missing function, then implement. This isn't the strict TDD assignment,
       so I won't stop separately at RED.
     - Verify, from ~/Documents/BorrowLoop:
       - npm test: all 190 existing tests plus the new ones pass.
       - npm run test:coverage: still 100%.
       - npm run lint, npx next typegen, npx tsc --noEmit, git diff --check.
       - git status --short: only the two files are changed.
     - Risks: small.
       - The decode-once and fail-closed choices are policy, and the tests
         document them.
       - When PR #36 fixes cuid IDs, communityIdSchema may tighten, and the
         matcher picks that up automatically.
     - Suggested commit: feat(auth): add pure community route matcher for member
       and admin paths.
     - Stop afterwards. I report the commands, their output and the changed
       files, and do no Git operations. Your branch and commits eeb931b…33146fa
       stay untouched.

                 |
     | GET /c/<id>/admin with no cookie                         | 307 to sign-in
     (unchanged) |

                 |

       28 +
       29 +/** Community-scoped routes live under `/c/<communityId>/...`.
          + */
       30 +export const COMMUNITY_ROUTE_PREFIX = "/c";
       31 +
       32 +const ADMIN_SEGMENT = "admin";
       33 +const MAX_PATH_LENGTH = 2048;
       34 +
       35 +/**
       36 + * The access a community route needs. Mirrors `AccessLevel` i
          +n
       37 + * `src/server/domain/access.ts`, which can't be imported here
          + because it's
       38 + * server-only and the proxy imports this module.
       39 + */
       40 +export type CommunityRouteLevel = "member" | "admin";
       41 +
       42 +export type CommunityRouteMatch =
       43 +  /** Not a community route. */
       44 +  | { kind: "none" }
       45 +  /** Under `/c/` but malformed. The caller should answer not
          +found. */
       46 +  | { kind: "invalid" }
       47 +  | { kind: "community"; communityId: string; level: Community
          +RouteLevel };
       48 +
       49 +/** Decodes one path segment, or returns `null` if its encodin
          +g is malformed. */
       50 +function decodeSegment(segment: string): string | null {
       51 +  try {
       52 +    return decodeURIComponent(segment);
       53 +  } catch {
       54 +    return null;
       55 +  }
       56 +}
       57 +
       58 +/**
       59 + * Classifies a request path for the community access check.
       60 + *
       61 + * Pure: it reads only the path, never the session or the data
          +base, and grants
       62 + * nothing by itself. Callers still have to check membership o
          +n the server.
       63 + *
       64 + * - Each segment is decoded once, as Next does when routing,
          +and the community
       65 + *   ID is validated after decoding.
       66 + * - Malformed paths under `/c/` are `invalid`, not `none`, so
          + a gate built on
       67 + *   this fails closed.
       68 + * - "admin" as the first segment after the ID makes it an adm
          +in route in any
       69 + *   letter case or encoding. Classifying more paths as admin
          +only makes the
       70 + *   check stricter.
       71 + */
       72 +export function matchCommunityPath(pathname: string): Communit
          +yRouteMatch {
       73 +  if (pathname !== COMMUNITY_ROUTE_PREFIX && !pathname.startsW
          +ith(`${COMMUNITY_ROUTE_PREFIX}/`)) {
       74 +    return { kind: "none" };
       75 +  }
       76 +  if (pathname.length > MAX_PATH_LENGTH) {
       77 +    return { kind: "invalid" };
       78 +  }
       79 +
       80 +  const rawSegments = pathname.slice(COMMUNITY_ROUTE_PREFIX.le
          +ngth + 1).split("/");
       81 +  // Allow a single trailing slash: "/c/abc/" is the same page
          + as "/c/abc".
       82 +  if (rawSegments.length > 1 && rawSegments.at(-1) === "") {
       83 +    rawSegments.pop();
       84 +  }
       85 +
       86 +  const segments: string[] = [];
       87 +  for (const rawSegment of rawSegments) {
       88 +    const segment = decodeSegment(rawSegment);
       89 +    if (segment === null || segment === "" || segment === "."
          +|| segment === "..") {
       90 +      return { kind: "invalid" };
       91 +    }
       92 +    segments.push(segment);
       93 +  }
       94 +
       95 +  const [rawId, firstSubpath] = segments;
       96 +  const id = communityIdSchema.safeParse(rawId);
       97 +  if (!id.success) {
       98 +    return { kind: "invalid" };
       99 +  }
      100 +
      101 +  const level: CommunityRouteLevel = firstSubpath?.toLowerCase
          +() === ADMIN_SEGMENT ? "admin" : "member";
      102 +  return { kind: "community", communityId: id.data, level };
      103 +}

  Checkpoint: CP-PRE. I worked on feature/us1-google-auth-community, starting
  from 33146fa with a clean tree. Your commits are untouched, nothing is staged,
  and no Git operations were run.

  What it does

  matchCommunityPath(pathname) reads only the path, never the session or
  database. It returns one of three results:
  - none: not a community route, for example /, /communities, /api/c/abc,
    /cx/abc or /C/abc.
  - invalid: under /c/ but malformed. The future proxy gate will answer these
    with 404 rather than let them through. This covers:
    - a missing or bad community ID (checked with the existing communityIdSchema
      after decoding)
    - an encoded slash in the ID (abc%2Fadmin)
    - empty segments in the middle of the path
    - segments that decode to . or ..
    - percent-encoding that can't be decoded
    - paths over 2048 characters
  - community: the decoded community ID plus a level:
    - member: /c/abc, /c/abc/, /c/abc/items/123, and paths like
      /c/abc/administrator or /c/abc/items/admin.
    - admin: "admin" as the first segment after the ID, in any capitalization or
      percent-encoding (/c/abc/ADMIN, /c/abc/%61dmin). Treating more paths as
      admin only makes the check stricter.

  src/proxy.ts is unchanged. Nothing imports the database or Prisma, and the
  schema, migrations, package.json, vitest.config.mts and CI are untouched.

● matchCommunityPath now returns invalid for any segment that decodes to contain
  / or \. All 245 tests pass, and coverage, lint and type checks are clean.
  Only the two proxy-rules files changed, and nothing is staged or committed.

  The change

  - src/lib/auth/proxy-rules.ts: each decoded segment is now tested against
    /[/\\]/.
    - This rejects %2F, %2f, %5C and %5c in any segment.
    - It also rejects a raw \. Browsers normally turn a raw backslash into /
      before sending, but other clients might not.
    - The doc comment now explains why.
  - src/lib/auth/proxy-rules.test.ts: 11 new cases.
    - Separators inside the community ID, with upper- and lowercase encodings.
    - admin%2Freports and admin%5Creports.
    - Attempts to hide admin behind encoded separators, such as
      items%2F..%2Fadmin and items%5C..%5Cadmin.
    - An encoded slash as a whole segment, separators deeper in the path, and a
      raw backslash.

  Before the fix, the 8 cases with a separator after the community ID failed,
  which confirms the gap was real. The 3 cases inside the ID already passed,
  because communityIdSchema doesn't allow /, \ or %.

  "Rejects malformed IDs, dot segments, and encoded path separators (%2F, %5C)
  as invalid so the future proxy gate fails closed."

~~~~

**Annotation (my approach):** When I reviewed the matcher, I wondered what would happen if someone put an encoded slash or backslash in a URL. I asked Claude to test `%2F` and `%5C` before we committed. Eight tests failed, so there really was a gap. We fixed it and got to 245 passing tests. This was one of the clearest times my review changed the code.

## 6. Persistent Google identity and database integration

### Prisma Account schema and additive migration planning

**Original Claude Code transcript — `BorrowLoop-US1-Claude-Session.txt`, lines 3,319–3,915**

~~~~text
> We are beginning CP-6 of the approved BorrowLoop US-1 plan, following
  Homework 3 Part 2's Explore → Plan → Implement → Commit workflow.

  The current branch is feature/us1-google-auth-community at merge commit
  1f5c258. Prisma PR #36 and CI PR #35 have both been integrated, and the
  working tree was clean after the merge.

  I have decided to own the authentication-specific Prisma schema changes in
  US-1, with Aarushi reviewing them through our normal PR process.

  For this checkpoint, focus ONLY on the minimum schema support needed for
  persistent Google account identity.

  Tasks:

  1. Inspect the current prisma/schema.prisma, src/auth.ts,
  src/server/auth/config.ts, and the generated Prisma client types.
  2. Verify compatibility between next-auth 5.0.0-beta.32,
  @auth/prisma-adapter, Prisma 7.10.1, and our custom Prisma client generator
  output.
  3. Determine the exact Account model fields, unique constraints, indexes, and
  User relation needed by the adapter.
  4. Verify that JWT sessions can be retained without introducing a Session
  table.
  5. Confirm that account linking uses Google's providerAccountId rather than
  automatically linking users solely by email.
  6. Identify any required changes to existing User fields.
  7. Design tests demonstrating that repeated sign-ins resolve to the same
  database user ID.
  8. Propose a minimal Prisma migration, with no destructive changes to
  existing data.

  Important restrictions:
  - Do not edit files or install dependencies yet.
  - Do not run migrations, seed scripts, or access Neon.
  - Do not modify Membership, Community, Item, or unrelated models.
  - Do not perform Git mutations.
  - Do not expose or read environment secrets.

  At the end, report:
  - Compatibility findings
  - Exact proposed schema changes
  - Migration strategy
  - Test strategy
  - Any technical risks
  - The smallest safe implementation checkpoint

  Stop for my review before making any changes.

  We will implement, test, and commit only after I approve your findings.

● I've finished the CP-6 investigation without changing anything: no repo files
  edited, nothing installed, no migrations run, no database or Neon connection,
  no secrets read, and no Git changes. The branch is still
  feature/us1-google-auth-community at 1f5c258 with a clean tree. Persistent
  Google identity needs one new Account table and a relation field on User. The
  migration is purely additive.

  Compatibility findings

  Question: @auth/prisma-adapter and next-auth beta.32
  Finding: The latest adapter, 2.11.3, depends on @auth/core 0.41.3, the same
    version next-auth beta.32 already installs, so npm will share one copy.
  How I checked: npm view, npm ls
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Question: Adapter and Prisma 7.10.1
  Finding: Its @prisma/client peer range starts at >=2.26.0, which 7.10.1
    satisfies. At runtime it never imports @prisma/client; it only calls
    user.* and account.* on the client we pass in, so our generated client
    works.
  How I checked: Read the adapter package, downloaded to the scratchpad but
    not installed
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Question: Our generated client output
  Finding: This is the catch. The adapter's type for its client argument comes
    from @prisma/client, which in Prisma 7 points at .prisma/client/default.
    That module doesn't exist, because our client is generated into
    src/generated/prisma. Because skipLibCheck is on, TypeScript should treat
    the argument as any. It should compile with no cast, but the argument
    won't be type-checked. I'll confirm with tsc once the package is
    installed.
  How I checked: Read the type files and node_modules
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Question: Keeping JWT sessions with no Session table
  Finding: Yes. With JWT sessions, @auth/core never calls createSession, and
    builds the token with sub: user.id, the database user ID. Our
    exposeSessionUserId already copies token.sub into the session, so
    session.ts needs no change.
  How I checked: @auth/core sign-in handler (handle-login.js lines 192–199,
    266–272) and callback/index.js line 76
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Question: How accounts are linked
  Finding: By Google account ID first. It looks up the (provider,
    providerAccountId) pair before anything else. If no account matches but a
    user with the same email exists, sign-in fails with OAuthAccountNotLinked,
    unless the provider sets allowDangerousEmailAccountLinking. The Google
    provider doesn't set it, and our config doesn't either. Google's
    providerAccountId is its sub claim, which never changes for an account.
  How I checked: handle-login.js lines 175–251, providers/google.js
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Question: What gets written to Account
  Finding: provider, type, providerAccountId and userId, plus Google's token
    fields: access_token, id_token, refresh_token, expires_at, scope,
    token_type, session_state. Every field sent needs a column, or Prisma
    rejects the write.
  How I checked: lib/utils/providers.js lines 92–102
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Question: Does the existing User need changes?
  Finding: Only the new relation field. The adapter creates users with name,
    email, image and emailVerified: null, and lets Prisma generate the ID,
    which stays a cuid. These fields already exist: email is required and
    unique, and Google always provides it. No column changes.
  How I checked: Schema and adapter createUser
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Question: Build without database variables
  Finding: Risk found. src/lib/db.ts creates the client as soon as it's
    imported and throws if DATABASE_URL is missing. Importing it from
    src/auth.ts would probably break builds without database variables,
    including CI. next-auth beta.32 supports passing a function instead of a
    config object (index.d.ts line 323), so creating the client lazily avoids
    this. Nothing imports db.ts yet.
  How I checked: Read the source

  Proposed schema change

  Two additions, with nothing removed or changed in existing models:

  prisma
  model User {
    // ...existing fields unchanged...
    accounts        Account[]      // new relation field only, no column
    memberships     Membership[]
    // ...
  }

  /// A sign-in method linked to a user (Auth.js). Users are found by
  /// (provider, providerAccountId), never by email alone. Field names follow
  the
  /// Auth.js adapter contract.
  model Account {
    id                String   @id @default(cuid())
    userId            String
    type              String
    provider          String
    providerAccountId String
    refresh_token     String?
    access_token      String?
    expires_at        Int?
    token_type        String?
    scope             String?
    id_token          String?
    session_state     String?
    createdAt         DateTime @default(now())
    updatedAt         DateTime @updatedAt

    user User @relation(fields: [userId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)

    @@unique([provider, providerAccountId])
    @@index([userId])
  }

  I wrote this change into a copy of the schema in the scratchpad, and npx
  prisma validate reports it as valid. Not included: Session, VerificationToken,
  Authenticator, and any change to Membership, Community, Item or other models.

  Migration strategy

  - Generated SQL. I produced it without a database, using npx prisma migrate
    diff --from-schema <current> --to-schema <proposed> --script on the two
    scratchpad files:
    - CREATE TABLE "Account", with a primary key and nullable token columns
    - CREATE INDEX "Account_userId_idx"
    - CREATE UNIQUE INDEX "Account_provider_providerAccountId_key"
    - a foreign key from "Account"."userId" to "User"("id"), with ON DELETE
      CASCADE
  - Nothing destructive. No existing table or column is altered, dropped or
    backfilled. Existing users and seed data are untouched.
  - File. prisma/migrations/<YYYYMMDDHHMMSS>_add_auth_account/migration.sql,
    generated with that diff command. I'd check it by running the diff again and
    confirming the output matches exactly. migration_lock.toml stays the same.
  - Applying it. I never apply it to Neon; applying it to the shared database is
    your team's call. There's no Docker or psql on this machine. npx prisma
    dev, part of the Prisma CLI we already have, can run a local throwaway
    Postgres for testing, but only if you approve.

  Test strategy

  1. No database needed: sign-in identity test.
     - Run the real PrismaAdapter against a small in-memory fake of user and
       account.
     - Follow @auth/core's sign-in steps: look up the account, find nothing,
       look up by email, find nothing, create the user, link the account.
     - A second sign-in with the same Google ID must return the same User.id.
     - That ID passed through exposeSessionUserId must give session.user.id ===
       User.id both times.
     - A different Google ID with the same email must not resolve to the
       existing user.
  2. No database needed: config tests.
     - The Google provider doesn't set allowDangerousEmailAccountLinking.
     - The session strategy is still jwt.
     - src/auth.ts passes PrismaAdapter(...) and creates the client lazily, so
       importing it never opens a database connection.
  3. Skipped unless TEST_DATABASE_URL is set (the same pattern as Aarushi's
     item-photos.test.ts). In prisma/account.test.ts, which the existing
     prisma/**/*.test.ts glob already picks up:
     - a repeated getUserByAccount returns the same ID
     - a duplicate (provider, providerAccountId) is rejected with P2002
     - deleting a user also deletes their accounts
  4. Manual check, which you'd run: sign in with Google twice against a local
     database, then confirm in Prisma Studio that there's one User row and one
     Account row with the same ID.
  5. Every checkpoint: npm test (all 245 US-1 tests and Aarushi's 11), npm run
     test:coverage, npm run lint, npx next typegen, npx tsc --noEmit, npm run
     build without a database URL, and npx prisma validate.

  Technical risks

  1. The adapter's client argument isn't type-checked. It compiles without a
     cast, but a field typo on our side wouldn't be caught there. The
     alternative is a hand-written typed adapter (about 50 lines, six methods,
     no new package). Decision A.
  2. Storing Google tokens. Google returns access and ID tokens. Storing them
     puts credentials in the database that BorrowLoop never uses. Passing an
     account() option to the Google provider that returns nothing would mean no
     tokens are stored; the columns stay, unused, for adapter compatibility.
     Decision B. I'll confirm that option is honored when I implement it.
  3. Build-time database import (above). Creating the client lazily handles it,
     and I'll confirm with a build that has no database URL.
  4. The seed's @example.com users have no Account. A real Google user with the
     same email would get OAuthAccountNotLinked. That's the safe outcome.
  5. Sessions issued before this change carry random IDs that match no user.
     They fail membership checks and get a 404, and nothing leaks.
  6. Testing without a database. No local Postgres exists. The database tests
     skip unless you approve prisma dev or provide a throwaway database URL.
  7. npm audit: the 3 high-severity effect advisories from Prisma 7, noted in
     CP-5, are still open.

  Smallest safe checkpoint

  CP-6, schema only: add the Account model and the accounts relation to
  prisma/schema.prisma, add the generated add_auth_account migration, and add
  the database-gated prisma/account.test.ts. That's three files.
  - No package installs and no changes to src/auth.ts or other auth code.
  - Not applied to any database.
  - Verified with prisma validate, prisma generate, a matching re-diff of the
    migration, and the full test, lint, tsc and build list.
  - Suggested commit: feat(db): add Auth.js Account model for stable Google
    identity.

  CP-7 then wires up the adapter: the lazy client, the adapter (A), the token
  decision (B), and the no-database identity tests.

  Decisions I need from you

  - A. Adapter: the official @auth/prisma-adapter@2.11.3, which needs install
    approval, with its client argument effectively untyped? Or a hand-written
    typed adapter with no new package? I lean toward the official adapter, since
    the identity test above runs the real adapter code.
  - B. Google tokens: don't store them (I recommend this), or store them in the
    nullable columns?
  - C. Local database for the gated tests: approve npx prisma dev as a throwaway
    local Postgres, or let those tests skip?

**Annotation (my approach):** I wanted to keep the database change as small as possible. For Google sign-in, we needed a way to link a Google account to the same user every time, so Claude checked the adapter and proposed an `Account` table. We reviewed the migration instead of changing other models or trying things on the shared database.

### Auth.js adapter implementation, tests, and build verification

> We are beginning CP-7 of the approved BorrowLoop US-1 implementation plan.

  Current branch: feature/us1-google-auth-community
  Current HEAD: 47392e9
  CP-6 is committed and pushed. The Account model, User.accounts relation, and
  additive migration are present.

  We are following Homework 3 Part 2: Explore → Plan → Implement → Commit. The
  overall exploration and plan are already complete.

  GOAL:
  Integrate Auth.js with Prisma so Google sign-ins resolve to persistent
  database User IDs, while preserving JWT sessions and existing authentication
  behavior.

  APPROVED DECISIONS:
  1. Prefer the official @auth/prisma-adapter@2.11.3.
  2. Keep next-auth 5.0.0-beta.32 and Prisma 7.10.1.
  3. Keep JWT sessions; do not add a Session table.
  4. Use the existing custom-generated Prisma client.
  5. Do not automatically link accounts by matching email addresses.
  6. Avoid persisting Google access, refresh, and ID tokens unless technically
  necessary.
  7. Do not modify the shared Neon database.

  IMPLEMENTATION WORKFLOW:

  Phase 1 — Verify compatibility:
  - Inspect the current Auth.js configuration, session callbacks, Prisma client
  factory, and Account model.
  - Install @auth/prisma-adapter@2.11.3 using npm.
  - Verify the official adapter's TypeScript compatibility with our
  custom-generated Prisma client.
  - Do not assume skipLibCheck makes incompatible client types safe.
  - If the official adapter cannot be integrated safely, stop and report the
  exact issue before implementing a custom adapter.

  Phase 2 — Integrate persistent identity:
  - Wire the Prisma adapter into Auth.js.
  - Ensure the Prisma client is initialized lazily so CI builds without
  DATABASE_URL still work.
  - Preserve JWT session strategy and existing session.user.id behavior.
  - Ensure returning Google users resolve to their original database User.id.
  - Keep Google provider account linking based on providerAccountId, not email
  alone.
  - Preserve existing redirect validation, route guards, and error handling.
  - Verify whether Auth.js supports suppressing unnecessary OAuth token
  persistence without disrupting account linking. If uncertain, stop and
  explain the tradeoff instead of guessing.
  - Do not introduce unrelated database schema changes.

  Phase 3 — Tests:
  - Add focused unit tests for persistent identity and adapter integration.
  - Test repeated sign-ins with the same Google providerAccountId.
  - Test different Google account IDs and existing-email conflicts.
  - Test that JWT session user IDs reflect persistent database IDs.
  - Test lazy initialization and no-database build compatibility.
  - Use mocks or an in-memory adapter-compatible test client where appropriate.
  - Do not claim mocked tests prove real PostgreSQL persistence.
  - Preserve all 245 original US-1 tests.

  Phase 4 — Validation:
  Run:
  - npm test
  - npm run test:coverage
  - npm run lint
  - npx next typegen
  - npx tsc --noEmit
  - npm run build
  - git diff --check

  Also verify that package-lock.json matches package.json and the production

● CP-7 is in: Google sign-ins now resolve to a permanent database User.id,
  sessions are still JWT with no Session table, and the build works without
  DATABASE_URL. The official adapter is wired in, but its client argument turned
  out to be untyped, so I added our own typed boundary around it (details
  below). Nothing has run against a real PostgreSQL database yet; the
  persistence tests use an in-memory stand-in. All changes are uncommitted.

  No real database, Neon or real credentials were used, no migration or seed
  ran, no .env* file was opened, and no mutating Git commands were run. prisma/
  and every model are unchanged.

  1. Files changed

  File: package.json, package-lock.json
  Change: Added "@auth/prisma-adapter": "2.11.3" at that exact version.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: src/server/db/client.ts (new)
  Change: getDb(): server-only. It creates the Prisma client on first call
    using Aarushi's createPrismaClient, then reuses it.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: src/server/auth/adapter.ts (new)
  Change: createAuthAdapter(getClient): a thin layer over the official
    PrismaAdapter that doesn't create the client until a method runs. It also
    has compile-time checks that the data shapes fit our schema.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: src/server/auth/config.ts
  Change: The Google provider is now Google({ account: discardOAuthTokens }).

    ID "temporary".

  ────────────────────────────────────────

  File: src/auth.test.ts (new)
  Change: 3 tests

  2. Adapter and Prisma compatibility

  - Versions: @auth/prisma-adapter@2.11.3 shares the same @auth/core@0.41.3 as
    next-auth beta.32, so there's one copy. Prisma stays at 7.10.1.
  - Its TypeScript type for our client is missing, not mismatched. I checked
    this with a temporary probe file, since deleted.
    - The adapter's client argument resolves to any.
    - With skipLibCheck turned off, tsc shows the cause: Module
      '"@prisma/client"' has no exported member 'PrismaClient'. Prisma 7's
      @prisma/client points at .prisma/client/default, which doesn't exist
      because our client is generated into src/generated/prisma.
  - How I made it safe instead of relying on skipLibCheck:
    - adapter.ts has compile-time assertions that AdapterUser without id fits
      Prisma.UserCreateInput, AdapterAccount fits
      Prisma.AccountUncheckedCreateInput, and the adapter's
      provider_providerAccountId key fits Prisma.AccountWhereUniqueInput.
    - I confirmed that a deliberately wrong shape makes tsc fail with TS2344.
    - At runtime the adapter never imports @prisma/client; it only calls user.*
      and account.* on the client we give it.

  3. How persistent identity works

  - On a new sign-in, Auth.js first looks up Account(provider "google",
    providerAccountId = Google's sub). If there's no match and no user with that
    email, it creates a User with a cuid ID and links an Account to it.
  - On later sign-ins, the same lookup finds that User. Google's sub never
    changes for an account, so the result stays the same even if the person's
    Google email changes.
  - The database client is created lazily. Importing src/auth.ts, building the
    config, and reading a JWT session never touch the database. The client is
    created only when Auth.js calls an adapter method during sign-in.
  - Only six adapter methods are exposed: createUser, getUser, getUserByEmail,
    getUserByAccount, updateUser, linkAccount. If someone ever switched to
    database sessions, Auth.js would refuse to start with MissingAdapterMethods,
    which I confirmed by reading its source.

  4. How JWT sessions keep the database ID

  - The strategy is still jwt. On sign-in, Auth.js builds the token with sub:
    user.id, which is now the database ID.
  - keepServerIssuedToken still ignores anything the client sends, and
    exposeSessionUserId still sets session.user.id from token.sub.
  - session.ts, the redirect validation, the proxy and the guards are unchanged.

  5. OAuth tokens and email-linking risk

  - No tokens are stored. The Google provider's account option is set to
    discardOAuthTokens, which returns {}.
    - Auth.js then saves only Object.assign({}, { provider, type,
      providerAccountId, userId }), confirmed in @auth/core's handle-login.js
      and utils/providers.js.
    - Linking uses only (provider, providerAccountId), so it's unaffected.
    - The tests confirm the saved account row contains only id, provider,
      providerAccountId, type and userId.
  - No linking by email. allowDangerousEmailAccountLinking isn't set. A
    different Google account that shares an existing user's email gets
    OAuthAccountNotLinked, and nothing is written.
  - Expected Auth.js behavior to be aware of: if a user is already signed in and
    then completes sign-in with a second, unlinked Google account, Auth.js
    links that account to the signed-in user. That requires being signed in
    first, which is how Auth.js intends it.

  6. Tests and coverage

  - npm test: 276 passed, 7 skipped (283), across 21 files.
    - All 245 original US-1 tests pass.
    - 23 new tests, all passing.
    - 8 Cloudinary tests pass.
    - 7 database tests skip without TEST_DATABASE_URL: 4 in
      prisma/account.test.ts and 3 in item-photos.
  - What the new tests show:
    - adapter.test.ts runs @auth/core's real handleLoginOrRegister together with
      the real PrismaAdapter, against an in-memory stand-in that enforces the
      same unique keys as the schema:
      - The first sign-in creates one user and one account, with no tokens.
      - Three sign-ins with the same Google ID give the same User.id.
      - The JWT-to-session steps give that same ID.
      - A changed Google email still resolves to the same user.
      - A different Google ID creates a separate user.
      - A different Google ID with an existing email is rejected with nothing
        written.
    - The lazy-creation tests confirm that importing src/auth.ts, creating the
      adapter, and importing getDb never create a client. A missing DATABASE_URL
      fails only when a method actually runs.
  - The security tests can actually fail. I temporarily broke config.ts and
    restored it byte for byte afterwards.
    - Removing the token option failed exactly the token test, and its diff
      listed the token fields that would have been stored.
    - Turning on email linking failed the two linking tests.
  - Coverage: 93.61% of statements (132/141), 92.3% of branches, 97.77% of
    functions. Every US-1 file is at 100%. Only main's database files remain
    below 100%.

  7. Build, lint and type check

  8. Still needs a real PostgreSQL test

  1. The in-memory tests don't prove real persistence. Real database behavior is
     covered by prisma/account.test.ts and an adapter run against the migrated
     schema, but neither has run yet. They need the disposable local prisma dev
     database you approved for later. One thing to confirm there: that Postgres
     accepts the account row with no token fields.
  2. A real Google sign-in. You'd sign in twice with your own credentials
     against the local database, then confirm in Prisma Studio there's one User
     and one Account with no tokens.
  3. The identity test imports Auth.js's sign-in function by file path, because
     @auth/core doesn't export it publicly. A version check fails loudly if
     next-auth stops pinning @auth/core@0.41.3.
  4. Test-only type casts: the in-memory stand-in and a partial options object
     are cast in tests, and each cast has a comment explaining why. There are no
     casts in app code.
  5. Still open:
     - Sessions issued before this change carry random IDs that match no user,
       so they fail membership checks.
     - The npm audit advisories in effect, which come from Prisma 7.

  Suggested commit message: feat(auth): persist Google users with stable Prisma
  IDs via the Auth.js adapter

~~~~

**Annotation (my approach):** This part connected Google sign-in to the database user ID. I paid attention to how accounts were linked because I didn't want two accounts matched just because they shared an email address. We also checked that the app could still build without live credentials and that the tests covered the adapter behavior.

### Local database tests and a server-binding security issue

  6. Identify the exact commands required to start the disposable database,

  - Do not read, display, or modify .env files or credentials.
